\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathfrak{m}}({\mathtt{r}}_{p-1}(x))=m^{-}_{p-1,{\mathtt{r}}_{p-1}(x)}

Clearly,
{\mathbb{P}}^{\epsilon}_{x}
-almost surely,

{\mathtt{x}}(\tau({\mathscr{M}}_{p-1}))={\mathfrak{m}}({\mathtt{l}}_{p-1}(x))
or

{\mathtt{x}}(\tau({\mathscr{M}}_{p-1}))={\mathfrak{m}}({\mathtt{r}}_{p-1}(x))
. By Proposition

7.5
, since
\vartheta_{\epsilon}\prec e^{{\mathfrak{h}}_{p}/\epsilon}
,
for
k\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}I_{x}:=\{{\mathtt{l}}_{p-1}(x),{\mathtt{r}}_{p-1}(x)\}}
,

\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}{\mathbb{P}}^{\epsilon}_{{\mathfrak{m}}(k)}\Big[\,\bigcap_{j=1}^{n}\Big\{\,{\mathtt{x}}(\theta_{\epsilon}^{(p)}t_{j}-\tau({\mathscr{M}}_{p-1}))\in{\mathscr{E}}({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k_{j}))\,\Big\}\,\Big]\,\,=\,{\mathtt{Q}}^{(p)}_{{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)}\big[\,\bigcap_{j=1}^{{{\mathfrak{n}}}}\big\{\,{\mathtt{x}}(t_{j})={\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k_{j})\,\}\,\,\big]\,.

Thus, the first term on the right-hand side of (
7.7
) is equal to

\sum_{k\in I_{x}}{\mathbb{P}}^{\epsilon}_{x}\big[\,\tau({\mathscr{M}}_{p-1})<\vartheta_{\epsilon}\,,\,{\mathtt{x}}(\tau({\mathscr{M}}_{p-1}))={\mathfrak{m}}(k)\,\big]\,{\mathtt{Q}}^{(p)}_{{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)}\big[\,\bigcap_{j=1}^{{{\mathfrak{n}}}}\big\{\,{\mathtt{x}}(t_{j})={\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k_{j})\,\}\,\,\big]\,+\,R^{(2)}_{\epsilon}(x)\,,

where (
7.3
) holds for
R=R^{(2)}
. By Lemma
4.3
, the
previous expression is equal to

\sum_{k\in I_{x}}{\mathbb{P}}^{\epsilon}_{x}\big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(\tau({\mathscr{M}}_{p-1}))={\mathfrak{m}}(k)\,\big]\,{\mathtt{Q}}^{(p)}_{{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)}\big[\,\bigcap_{j=1}^{{{\mathfrak{n}}}}\big\{\,{\mathtt{x}}(t_{j})={\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k_{j})\,\}\,\,\big]\,+\,R^{(3)}_{\epsilon}(x)\,,

where (
7.3
) holds for
R=R^{(3)}
. Recall the definition of the
measure
{\mathtt{h}}_{p}(x,\cdot)
introduced in (
2.27
). By Corollary

4.9
, the previous expression is equal to

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in I_{x}}{\mathtt{h}}_{p}(x,{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k))\,{\mathtt{Q}}^{(p)}_{{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)}\big[\,\bigcap_{j=1}^{{{\mathfrak{n}}}}\big\{\,{\mathtt{x}}(t_{j})={\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k_{j})\,\}\,\,\big]\,+\,R^{(4)}_{\epsilon}(x)

\displaystyle=\,{\mathtt{Q}}^{(p)}_{{\mathtt{h}}_{p}(x,\cdot)}\big[\,\bigcap_{j=1}^{{{\mathfrak{n}}}}\big\{\,{\mathtt{x}}(t_{j})={\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k_{j})\,\}\,\,\big]\,+\,R^{(4)}_{\epsilon}(x)\,,

where (
7.3
) holds for
R=R^{(4)}
. This completes the proof of
the theorem. ∎

8.
Proof of Theorem
2.1
and
2.5

The proof relies on Lemma
8.2
below, which refines Proposition

7.5
. The first result is a simple consequence of the formula
(
2.22
) for the jump rates of the Markov chain
{\mathbb{X}}_{p}(\cdot)
,
and the fact that the jumps are strictly positive among the
nearest-neighbours of a
{\mathbb{X}}_{p}(\cdot)
-closed irreducible class.

Lemma 8.1
.

Fix
1\leq p<{\mathfrak{q}}
,
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. Denote by
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(i)
,

\ell_{1}\leq i\leq\ell_{2}
, the sets forming the
{\mathbb{X}}_{p}
-closed
irreducible class
{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k)
. Then, the
stationary state of the Markov chain
{\mathbb{X}}_{p}(\cdot)
restricted to

{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k)
is given by
\pi({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(i))/\pi({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k))
,

\ell_{1}\leq i\leq\ell_{2}
.

The proof of the next lemma is similar to the one of
[
19
, Lemma
12.8]
. We present it here for completeness.

Lemma 8.2
.

Fix
1\leq p\leq{\mathfrak{q}}
,
j
,
t>0
,
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
and
m\in{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)
.
Then, for any sequence
(\vartheta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}:\epsilon>0)
such
that
\vartheta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}\prec\theta_{\epsilon}^{(p)}
,

\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\,\sup_{x\in\mathcal{E}({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(j))}\,\sup_{|s|\leq\vartheta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}}\,\Big|\,\mathbb{P}_{x}^{\epsilon}\big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(\theta_{\epsilon}^{(p)}t+s)\in\mathcal{E}(m)\,\big]\,-\,\frac{\pi(m)}{\pi({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k))}\,{\mathtt{Q}}_{{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(j)}^{(p)}\big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(t)={\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)\,\big]\,\Big|\ =\ 0\,.

Proof.

The proof is by induction. For
p=1
, as the sets

{\mathscr{M}}_{1}(\ell)
are singletons, the assertion of the lemma
corresponds to the one of Proposition
7.5
.

Fix
p\geq 2
and assume that the lemma holds for
1\leq p^{\prime}<p
. Fix

k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,
m\in{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)
and a sequence

(\vartheta^{(p)}_{\epsilon})_{\epsilon>0}
such that

\vartheta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}\prec\theta_{\epsilon}^{(p)}
. By
construction,

{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)=\cup_{\ell_{1}\leq i\leq\ell_{2}}{\mathscr{M}}_{p-1}(i)
for some

\ell_{1}\leq\ell_{2}
. Denote by
i_{0}\in\{\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{2}\}
the
index such that
m\in{\mathscr{M}}_{p-1}(i_{0})
.

Fix
\eta>0
. Since

\{{\mathscr{M}}_{p-1}(i):\ell_{1}\leq i\leq\ell_{2}\}
forms a

{\mathbb{X}}_{p-1}
-recurrent class, by the paragraph preceding the
statement of the lemma, there exists
s_{0}=s_{0}(\eta)>0
such that

\max_{\ell_{1}\leq i\leq\ell_{2}}\,\Big|\,{\mathtt{Q}}_{{\mathscr{M}}_{p-1}(i)}^{(p-1)}\big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(s_{0})={\mathscr{M}}_{p-1}(i_{0})\,\big]\,-\,\frac{\pi({\mathscr{M}}_{p-1}(i_{0}))}{\pi({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k))}\,\Big|\,\leq\,\eta\,.

(8.1)

Fix
x\in{\mathscr{E}}({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k))
,
|s|\leq\vartheta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}
,
and write

{\mathbb{P}}_{x}^{\epsilon}\big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(\theta_{\epsilon}^{(p)}t+s)\in{\mathscr{E}}(m)\,\big]\,=\,\sum_{j=1}^{3}{\mathbb{P}}_{x}^{\epsilon}\big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(\theta_{\epsilon}^{(p)}t+s)\in{\mathscr{E}}(m)\,\big|\,\mathscr{A}_{j}\,\big]\,{\mathbb{P}}_{x}^{\epsilon}[\,\mathscr{A}_{j}\,]\;,

where

\displaystyle\mathscr{A}_{1}\ =\ \{\,{\mathtt{x}}(t_{\epsilon})\in{\mathscr{E}}({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k))\,\}\;,\quad\mathscr{A}_{2}\ =\ \{\,{\mathtt{x}}(t_{\epsilon})\in{\mathscr{E}}({\mathscr{M}}_{p})\setminus{\mathscr{E}}({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k))\,\}\;,\quad\mathscr{A}_{3}\ =\ \{\,{\mathtt{x}}(t_{\epsilon})\not\in{\mathscr{E}}({\mathscr{M}}_{p})\,\}\;,

and
t_{\epsilon}=\theta_{\epsilon}^{(p)}t+s-\theta_{\epsilon}^{(p-1)}s_{0}
.

We claim that the term corresponding to
\mathscr{A}_{2}
is
negligible. Bound
{\mathbb{P}}_{x}^{\epsilon}[\,\mathscr{A}_{2}\,]
by

1
and apply the strong Markov property to get that this term is less
than or equal to

\sup_{k^{\prime}\neq k}\,\sup_{y\in{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k^{\prime})}\,\mathbb{P}_{y}^{\epsilon}\big[\,\tau_{{\mathscr{E}}({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k))}<\theta_{\epsilon}^{(p-1)}s_{0}\,\big]\;.

By Corollary
7.3
this expression vanishes as
\epsilon\to 0
.

By (
7.5
), the term corresponding to
\mathscr{A}_{3}
is also
negligible. We turn to the term corresponding to

\mathscr{A}_{1}
. We estimate the two probabilities separately. For
the first probability, by the strong Markov property,

\displaystyle\sup_{|s|\leq\vartheta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}}\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{P}}_{x}^{\epsilon}\big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(\theta_{\epsilon}^{(p)}t+s)\in{\mathscr{E}}(m)\,\big|\,\mathscr{A}_{1}\,\big]\,-\,\frac{\pi(m)}{\pi({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k))}\,\Big|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sup_{y\in{\mathscr{E}}({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k))}\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{P}}_{y}^{\epsilon}\big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(\theta_{\epsilon}^{(p-1)}s_{0})\in{\mathscr{E}}(m)\,\big]\,-\,\frac{\pi(m)}{\pi({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k))}\,\Big|\;.

This expression is less than or equal to

\displaystyle\max_{\ell_{1}\leq i\leq\ell_{2}}\,\sup_{y\in{\mathscr{E}}({\mathscr{M}}_{p-1}(i))}\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{P}}_{y}^{\epsilon}\big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(\theta_{\epsilon}^{(p-1)}s_{0})\in{\mathscr{E}}(m)\,\big]\,-\,\frac{\pi(m)}{\pi({\mathscr{M}}_{p-1}(i_{0}))}\,{\mathtt{Q}}^{(p-1)}_{{\mathscr{M}}_{p-1}(i)}\big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(s_{0})={\mathscr{M}}_{p-1}(i_{0})\,\big]\,\Big|

\displaystyle\quad+\,\max_{\ell_{1}\leq i\leq\ell_{2}}\,\sup_{y\in{\mathscr{E}}({\mathscr{M}}_{p-1}(i))}\,\Big|\,\frac{\pi(m)}{\pi({\mathscr{M}}_{p-1}(i_{0}))}\,{\mathtt{Q}}^{(p-1)}_{{\mathscr{M}}_{p-1}(i)}\big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(s_{0})={\mathscr{M}}_{p-1}(i_{0})\,\big]\,-\,\frac{\pi(m)}{\pi({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k))}\,\Big|\;.

By the induction hypothesis, the first term converges to
0
as

\epsilon\to 0
. Therefore, by (
8.1
),

\limsup_{\epsilon\to 0}\sup_{x\in\mathcal{E}({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(j))}\sup_{|s|\leq\vartheta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}}\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{P}}_{x}^{\epsilon}\big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(\theta_{\epsilon}^{(p)}t+s)\in{\mathscr{E}}(m)\,\big|\,\mathscr{A}_{1}\,\big]\,-\,\frac{\pi(m)}{\pi({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k))}\,\Big|\,\leq\,\eta\;.

For the second probability, by Proposition
7.5
,

\limsup_{\epsilon\to 0}\sup_{x\in\mathcal{E}({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(j))}\sup_{|s|\leq\vartheta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}}\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{P}}_{x}^{\epsilon}\big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(t_{\epsilon})\in{\mathscr{E}}({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k))\,\big]\,-\,{\mathtt{Q}}^{(p-1)}_{{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(j)}\big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(s_{0})={\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)\,\big]\,\Big|\,=\,0\;.

To complete the proof of the induction step and the one of the lemma,
it remains to recollect all the previous estimates, letting
\eta\to 0

at the end.
∎

Proof of Theorem
2.1
and
2.5
.
(a)
.

Fix a bounded, continuous function
u_{0}\colon{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}
,

x\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
t>0
,
1\leq p\leq{\mathfrak{q}}
, and
\delta>0
. Let

{\mathfrak{m}}_{p,{\mathtt{l}}}(x)
,
{\mathfrak{m}}_{p,{\mathtt{r}}}(x)
be the
S
-local minima
given by

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathfrak{m}}_{p,{\mathtt{l}}}(x)}\,:=\,\max\{m\in{\mathscr{M}}_{p}:m\leq x\,\}\,,\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathfrak{m}}_{p,{\mathtt{r}}}(x)}\,:=\,\min\{m\in{\mathscr{M}}_{p}:m\geq x\,\}\,,

(8.2)

and let
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{k}}_{p,{\mathtt{l}}}(x)
,

\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{k}}_{p,{\mathtt{r}}}(x)
be the integers such that

{\mathfrak{m}}_{p,{\mathtt{l}}}(x)\in{\mathscr{M}}_{p}({\mathtt{k}}_{p,{\mathtt{l}}}(x))
,

{\mathfrak{m}}_{p,{\mathtt{r}}}(x)\in{\mathscr{M}}_{p}({\mathtt{k}}_{p,{\mathtt{r}}}(x))
. In the
notation introduced right before the statement of Theorem
2.5
,

{\mathtt{k}}_{p,{\mathtt{l}}}(x)={\mathtt{l}}_{p}(x)
,

{\mathtt{k}}_{p,{\mathtt{r}}}(x)={\mathtt{r}}_{p}(x)

By the stochastic representation of the solution of the parabolic
equation (
1.3
),
[
12
, Theorem 6.5.3]
,

u(t\theta_{\epsilon}^{(p)},x)\;=\;{\mathbb{E}}^{p,\epsilon}_{x}\big[\,u_{0}({\mathtt{x}}(t))\,\big]\;.

(8.3)

Step 1: Reduction to
x\in{\mathscr{M}}_{p}
.
Fix a
time-scale
\vartheta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}\prec\theta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}
.
Repeat the arguments presented in the proof of Theorem
3.2
,
introducing the restriction

\{\tau({\mathscr{M}}_{p})<\vartheta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}\}
, and then

\{{\mathtt{x}}(\tau({\mathscr{M}}_{p}))=m\}
,
m={\mathfrak{m}}_{p,{\mathtt{l}}}(x)
,

{\mathfrak{m}}_{p,{\mathtt{r}}}(x)
(see equation (
7.7
), and below), to obtain that
(
8.3
) is equal to

\sum_{m\in M_{p}(x)}{\mathbb{E}}^{\epsilon}_{x}\Big[\,{\mathtt{1}}\big\{\tau({\mathscr{M}}_{p})<\vartheta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}\,,\,{\mathtt{x}}(\tau({\mathscr{M}}_{p}))=m\big\}\,{\mathbb{E}}^{\epsilon}_{m}\big[u_{0}({\mathtt{x}}(t\theta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}-\tau({\mathscr{M}}_{p})))\,\big]\,\Big]\,+\,R^{(1)}_{\epsilon}(x)\;,

(8.4)

where
R^{(1)}_{\epsilon}(x)
satisfies (
7.3
),

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}M_{p}(x)}:=\{{\mathfrak{m}}_{p,{\mathtt{l}}}(x),{\mathfrak{m}}_{p,{\mathtt{r}}}(x)\}
,

Step 2: Metastability.
It remains to
estimate

{\mathbb{E}}^{\epsilon}_{m}\big[u_{0}({\mathtt{x}}(t\theta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}-s))\,\big]
uniformly on
|s|\leq\vartheta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}
for

m\in M_{p}(x)
.

Fix
\delta>0
. By postulate
{\mathcal{P}}_{9}(p)
, the jump rates
R_{p}
are
periodic. Thus, the holding times at each state
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(j)
is
uniformly bounded. As the Markov chain
{\mathbb{X}}_{p}(\cdot)
jumps only to
nearest-neighbour sites, there exists, therefore,

\ell_{0}=\ell_{0}(t,{\mathbb{X}}_{p}(\cdot))
such that

\sum_{j:|j-k|\geq\ell_{0}}p^{(p)}_{t}({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k),{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(j))\,\leq\,\delta\quad\text{for all}\;\;k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\;.

(8.5)

Let
k_{m}={\mathtt{k}}_{p,{\mathtt{l}}}(x)
, if
m={\mathfrak{m}}_{p,{\mathtt{l}}}(x)
and

k_{m}={\mathtt{k}}_{p,{\mathtt{r}}}(x)
, if
m={\mathfrak{m}}_{p,{\mathtt{r}}}(x)
. Recall
from (
3.2
) the definition of the wells
{\mathscr{E}}(m)
, and choose

r_{0}
small enough so that

\max_{j:|j-k_{m}|\leq\ell_{0}}\sup_{y\in{\mathscr{E}}(m_{j})}|u_{0}(y)-u_{0}(m_{j})|\,\leq\,\delta\;.

By the previous estimate, for each
m\in M_{p}(x)
,

{\mathbb{E}}^{\epsilon}_{m}\big[u_{0}({\mathtt{x}}(t\theta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}-s))\,\big]\,=\,\sum_{j:|j-k_{m}|\leq\ell_{0}}\sum_{m^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(j)}u_{0}(m^{\prime})\,{\mathbb{P}}^{\epsilon}_{m}\big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(t\theta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}-s)\in{\mathscr{E}}(m^{\prime})\,\big]\,+\,R^{(2)}_{\epsilon}\,+\,R_{\delta}\;,

where
|R_{\delta}|\leq\delta
, and

|R^{(2)}_{\epsilon}|\,\leq\,\|u_{0}\|_{\infty}\;{\mathbb{P}}^{\epsilon}_{m}\Big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(t\theta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}-s)\not\in\bigcup_{j:|j-k_{m}|\leq\ell_{0}}{\mathscr{E}}({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(j))\,\Big]\;.

By Proposition
7.5
and (
8.5
),

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\sup_{|s|\leq\vartheta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}}{\mathbb{P}}^{\epsilon}_{m}\Big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(t\theta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}-s)\not\in\bigcup_{j:|j-k_{m}|\leq\ell_{0}}{\mathscr{E}}({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(j))\,\Big]

\displaystyle=\,\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\sup_{|s|\leq\vartheta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}}\Big\{\,1\,-\,{\mathbb{P}}^{\epsilon}_{m}\Big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(t\theta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}-s)\in\bigcup_{j:|j-k_{m}|\leq\ell_{0}}{\mathscr{E}}({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(j))\,\Big]\,\Big\}\,\leq\,\delta\;.

Therefore, by Lemma
8.2
,

\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\sup_{|s|\leq\vartheta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}}\Big|\,{\mathbb{E}}^{\epsilon}_{m}\big[u_{0}({\mathtt{x}}(t\theta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}-s))\,\big]-\,U_{p}(t,k_{m},\ell_{0})\,\,\Big|\,\leq\,\delta\,(1+\|u_{0}\|_{\infty})\;.

where

U_{p}(t,k_{m},\ell_{0})\,:=\,\sum_{j:|j-k_{m}|\leq\ell_{0}}{\mathtt{Q}}_{{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k_{m})}^{(p)}\big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(t)={\mathscr{M}}_{p}(j)\,\big]\sum_{m^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(j)}u_{0}(m^{\prime})\,\frac{\pi(m^{\prime})}{\pi({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(j))}

(8.6)

In view of (
8.5
), paying an extra

\delta\,\|u_{0}\|_{\infty}
, we may replace in the penultimate
formula
U_{p}(t,k_{m},\ell_{0})
by
U_{p}(t,k_{m})
, where this later
function is defined as in the previous displayed equation, but with
the sum running over
j\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.

Step 3: Conclusion.
In view of the previous
estimate, (
8.4
) is equal to

\sum_{m\in M_{p}(x)}U_{p}(t,k_{m})\,{\mathbb{P}}^{\epsilon}_{x}\big[\,\tau({\mathscr{M}}_{p})<\vartheta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}\,,\,{\mathtt{x}}(\tau({\mathscr{M}}_{p}))=m\,\big]\,+\,R^{(3)}_{\epsilon,\delta}(x)\;,

where

\limsup_{\epsilon\to 0}|\,R^{(3)}_{\epsilon,\delta}(x)\,|\,\leq\,(1+2\|u_{0}\|_{\infty})\,\delta\;.

By Lemma
4.3
we may remove the indicator of the event

\{\tau({\mathscr{M}}_{p})<\vartheta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}\}
, provided we choose a
sequence
\vartheta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}
such that

\vartheta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}\succ\epsilon^{-1}\,\theta^{(p-1)}_{\epsilon}
. This can be done since the only restriction
required up to this point is that

\vartheta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}\prec\theta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}
. To complete
the proof of the theorem, it remains to apply Corollary
4.9
and
to let
\delta\to 0
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
2.5
.(b)

The Theorem
2.5
for intermediate scales proof’s idea is quite
simple. In a time scale of order

\theta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}\prec\varrho_{\epsilon}\prec\theta^{(p+1)}_{\epsilon}
, the process reaches a set
{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k)

for some
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
(actually, the closest one to the left or right
of the starting point) and is not able to leave this set because

\varrho_{\epsilon}\prec\theta^{(p+1)}_{\epsilon}
. Since

\varrho_{\epsilon}\succ\theta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}
, and
{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k)

is a
{\mathbb{X}}_{p}
-closed irreducible class, the distribution of

X_{\epsilon}(\varrho_{\epsilon})
among the wells
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(j)
which
compose
{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k)
will be given by the stationary state of the
reduced Markov chain
{\mathbb{X}}_{p}(\cdot)
restricted to the set

{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k)
. The same argument may be applied to the distribution
inside the well
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(j)
, and an inductive argument permits to
express the distribution of
X_{\epsilon}(\varrho_{\epsilon})
among the
wells
{\mathscr{E}}(m)
,
m\in{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k)
, in terms of the stationary
states of the Markov chains
{\mathbb{X}}_{1}(\cdot),\dots,{\mathbb{X}}_{p}(\cdot)
.

Next result follows from the previous lemma, which states that the set

{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}
is reached in times of order
\theta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}
,
and Corollary
4.7
, which asserts that starting from

{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}
a time-scale or order

\theta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}\,e^{\kappa_{0}/\epsilon}
is required to attain
the set
{\mathscr{E}}_{p}\setminus{\mathscr{E}}_{p+1}
.

Corollary 8.3
.

Fix
1\leq p<{\mathfrak{q}}
, and sequences
\varrho_{\epsilon}
,

\vartheta_{\epsilon}
such that

\theta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}\prec\varrho_{\epsilon}
,

\vartheta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}\prec\varrho_{\epsilon}
. Then,

\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}}\sup_{|s|\leq\vartheta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}}{\mathbb{P}}^{\epsilon}_{x}\big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(\varrho_{\epsilon}-s)\in{\mathscr{E}}_{p}\setminus{\mathscr{E}}_{p+1}\,\big]\,=\,0\;.

Proof.

Recall the definition of
\kappa_{0}
introduced in Corollary
4.7
,
and fix
\delta>0
. By Corollary
7.4
, we may choose
t>0
,

\epsilon_{0}>0
such
that

\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}}{\mathbb{P}}^{\epsilon}_{x}\big[\,\tau({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1})>t\,\theta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}\,\big]\,\leq\,\delta

for all
0<\epsilon<\epsilon_{0}
.

Fix
|s|\leq\vartheta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}
. If

\varrho_{\epsilon}-s\leq e^{\kappa_{0}/\epsilon}\,\theta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}
we estimate the probability appearing in the
statement of the corollary by

{\mathbb{P}}^{\epsilon}_{x}\big[\,\tau({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1})<t\,\theta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}\,,\,{\mathtt{x}}(\varrho_{\epsilon}-s)\in{\mathscr{E}}_{p}\setminus{\mathscr{E}}_{p+1}\,\big]\,+\,\delta\;.

By the strong Markov property, and since

\varrho_{\epsilon}-s\leq e^{\kappa_{0}/\epsilon}\,\theta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}
, the probability appearing in this expression
is bounded by

\sup_{m\in{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}}\sup_{0\leq s^{\prime}\leq t\theta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}}{\mathbb{P}}^{\epsilon}_{m}\big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(\varrho_{\epsilon}-s-s^{\prime})\in{\mathscr{E}}_{p}\setminus{\mathscr{E}}_{p+1}\,\big]\,\leq\,\sup_{m\in{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}}{\mathbb{P}}^{\epsilon}_{m}\big[\,\tau({\mathscr{E}}_{p}\setminus{\mathscr{E}}_{p+1})\leq e^{\kappa_{0}/\epsilon}\,\theta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}\,\big]\;.

By Corollary
4.7
, this expression is bounded by
C_{0}\,\epsilon\,e^{-\kappa_{0}/\epsilon}
.

If

\varrho_{\epsilon}-s>e^{\kappa_{0}/\epsilon}\,\theta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}
we first apply the Markov property (at time

\varrho_{\epsilon}-s-e^{\kappa_{0}/\epsilon}
) to estimate the
probability appearing in the statement of the corollary by

\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}}{\mathbb{P}}^{\epsilon}_{x}\big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(e^{\kappa_{0}/\epsilon}\,\theta^{(p)}_{\epsilon})\in{\mathscr{E}}_{p}\setminus{\mathscr{E}}_{p+1}\,\big]\;.

Then, we repeat the previous argument.
∎

Lemma 8.4
.

Fix
1\leq p<{\mathfrak{q}}
,
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, and sequences
\varrho_{\epsilon}
,

\vartheta_{\epsilon}
such that

\theta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}\prec\varrho_{\epsilon}\prec\theta^{(p+1)}_{\epsilon}
,
\vartheta_{\epsilon}\prec\varrho_{\epsilon}
.
Then,

\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\sup_{|s|\leq\vartheta_{\epsilon}}\sup_{x\in{\mathscr{E}}({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k))}\Big|\,{\mathbb{P}}^{\epsilon}_{x}\big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(\varrho_{\epsilon}+s)\in{\mathscr{E}}(m^{\prime})\,\big]\,-\,\frac{\pi(m^{\prime})}{\pi({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k))}\,\Big|\,=\,0

for all
m^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k)
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to the one of Lemma
8.2
. Fix
\delta>0
,

k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,
x\in{\mathscr{E}}({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k))
. Assume that

{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k)=\cup_{\ell_{1}\leq i\leq\ell_{2}}{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(i)
. By
Lemma
8.1
, there exists
t_{0}=t_{0}(\delta)>0
such that

\max_{\ell_{1}\leq i,j\leq\ell_{2}}\,\Big|\,{\mathtt{Q}}^{(p)}_{{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(i)}\big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(t)={\mathscr{M}}_{p}(j)\,\big]\,-\,\frac{\pi({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(j))}{\pi({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k))}\,\Big|\,\leq\,\delta

for all
t\geq t_{0}
.

Fix
m^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k)
. Write the probability appearing in the
lemma as

\sum_{j=1}^{2}{\mathbb{P}}_{x}^{\epsilon}\big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(\varrho_{\epsilon}+s)\in{\mathscr{E}}(m^{\prime})\,\big|\,\mathscr{A}_{j}\,\big]\,{\mathbb{P}}_{x}^{\epsilon}[\,\mathscr{A}_{j}\,]\;,

where

{\mathscr{A}}_{1}\,=\,\{{\mathtt{x}}(t_{\epsilon})\in{\mathscr{E}}({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k))\}\,,\quad{\mathscr{A}}_{2}\,=\,\{{\mathtt{x}}(t_{\epsilon})\not\in{\mathscr{E}}({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k))\}\,,

and
t_{\epsilon}=\varrho_{\epsilon}+s-\theta_{\epsilon}^{(p)}t_{0}
.

As
\varrho_{\epsilon}\succ\theta_{\epsilon}^{(p)}
, by (
7.5
) (to
estimate the probability of the event

\{{\mathtt{x}}(t_{\epsilon})\not\in{\mathscr{E}}_{p}\}
), and Corollary
7.4

and Lemma
4.3
(to estimate the probability of the event

\{{\mathtt{x}}(t_{\epsilon})\in{\mathscr{E}}_{p}\setminus{\mathscr{E}}_{p+1}\}
),

\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\sup_{|s|\leq\vartheta_{\epsilon}}\sup_{x\in{\mathscr{E}}({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k))}{\mathbb{P}}_{x}^{\epsilon}[\,{\mathtt{x}}(t_{\epsilon})\not\in{\mathscr{E}}_{p+1}\,]\,=\,0\;.

Hence, as
\varrho_{\epsilon}\prec\theta_{\epsilon}^{(p+1)}
, by
Corollary
7.3
,

\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\sup_{|s|\leq\vartheta_{\epsilon}}\sup_{x\in{\mathscr{E}}({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k))}{\mathbb{P}}_{x}^{\epsilon}[\,\mathscr{A}_{2}\,]\,=\,0\;.

We turn to the first term. By the previous remark,

{\mathbb{P}}_{x}^{\epsilon}[\,\mathscr{A}_{1}\,]
converges to
1
, uniformly
on
x\in{\mathscr{E}}({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k))
,
|s|\leq\vartheta_{\epsilon}
. On the
other hand, we claim that

\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\sup_{x\in{\mathscr{E}}({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k))}\sup_{|s|\leq\vartheta_{\epsilon}}\Big|\,{\mathbb{P}}_{x}^{\epsilon}\big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(\varrho_{\epsilon}+s)\in{\mathscr{E}}(m^{\prime})\,\big|\,\mathscr{A}_{1}\,\big]\,-\,\frac{\pi(m^{\prime})}{\pi({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k))}\,\Big|\,=\,0\;.

Indeed, by the Markov property, this expression is bounded by

\max_{\ell_{1}\leq i\leq\ell_{2}}\sup_{y\in{\mathscr{E}}({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(i))}\Big|\,{\mathbb{P}}_{y}^{\epsilon}\big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(\theta_{\epsilon}^{(p)}\,t_{0})\in{\mathscr{E}}(m^{\prime})\,\big]\,-\,\frac{\pi(m^{\prime})}{\pi({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k))}\,\Big|\;.

Choose
\ell_{1}\leq i_{0}\leq\ell_{2}
such that
m^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(i_{0})
, and bound
the previous expression by

\displaystyle\max_{\ell_{1}\leq i\leq\ell_{2}}\sup_{y\in{\mathscr{E}}({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(i))}\Big|\,{\mathbb{P}}_{y}^{p,\epsilon}\big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(t_{0})\in{\mathscr{E}}(m^{\prime})\,\big]\,-\,{\mathtt{Q}}^{p}_{{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(i)}\big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(t_{0})={\mathscr{M}}_{p}(i_{0})\,]\,\frac{\pi(m^{\prime})}{\pi({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(i_{0}))}\,\Big|

\displaystyle+\,\max_{\ell_{1}\leq i\leq\ell_{2}}\Big|\,{\mathtt{Q}}^{p}_{{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(i)}\big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(t_{0})={\mathscr{M}}_{p}(i_{0})\,]\,\frac{\pi(m^{\prime})}{\pi({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(i_{0}))}\,-\,\frac{\pi(m^{\prime})}{\pi({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k))}\,\Big|

By the choice of
t_{0}
, the second term is bounded by
\delta
.
By Lemma
8.2
, the first one vanishes as
\epsilon\to 0
. This
completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Proof of Theorem
2.5
.
(b)
.

Fix a bounded, continuous function
u_{0}\colon{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}
,

x\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
1\leq p<{\mathfrak{q}}
, a sequence
\varrho_{\epsilon}

such that

\theta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}\prec\varrho_{\epsilon}\prec\theta^{(p+1)}_{\epsilon}
, and
\delta>0
.
By the stochastic representation of the solution of the parabolic
equation (
1.3
),
[
12
, Theorem 6.5.3]
,

u(\varrho_{\epsilon},x)\;=\;{\mathbb{E}}^{\epsilon}_{x}\big[\,u_{0}({\mathtt{x}}(\varrho_{\epsilon}))\,\big]\;.

(8.7)

The proof is similar to the one of Theorem
2.5
.(a). Recall
the definition of the
S
-local minima
{\mathfrak{m}}_{p+1,{\mathtt{l}}}(x)
,

{\mathfrak{m}}_{p+1,{\mathtt{r}}}(x)
introduced in (
8.2
). Fix a time-scale

\theta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}\prec\vartheta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}\prec\varrho_{\epsilon}
. Repeat the arguments presented in the proof of
Theorem
3.2
, introducing the restriction

\{\tau({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1})<\vartheta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}\}
, and then

\{{\mathtt{x}}(\tau({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}))=m\}
,
m={\mathfrak{m}}_{p+1,{\mathtt{l}}}(x)
,

{\mathfrak{m}}_{p+1,{\mathtt{r}}}(x)
, to obtain that (
8.7
) is equal to

\sum_{m\in M_{p+1}(x)}{\mathbb{E}}^{\epsilon}_{x}\Big[\,{\mathtt{1}}\big\{\tau({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1})<\vartheta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}\,,\,{\mathtt{x}}(\tau({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}))=m\big\}\,{\mathbb{E}}^{\epsilon}_{m}\big[u_{0}({\mathtt{x}}(\varrho_{\epsilon}-\tau({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1})))\,\big]\,\Big]\,+\,R^{(1)}_{\epsilon}(x)\;,

(8.8)

where

M_{p+1}(x)=\{{\mathfrak{m}}_{p+1,{\mathtt{l}}}(x),{\mathfrak{m}}_{p+1,{\mathtt{r}}}(x)\}
. By
Corollary
7.4
,
R^{(1)}_{\epsilon}(x)
satisfies (
7.3
).

Let
k_{m}={\mathtt{k}}_{p+1,{\mathtt{l}}}(x)
if
m={\mathfrak{m}}_{p+1,{\mathtt{l}}}(x)
, and

k_{m}={\mathtt{k}}_{p+1,{\mathtt{r}}}(x)
if
m={\mathfrak{m}}_{p+1,{\mathtt{r}}}(x)
. Recall
from (
3.2
) the definition of the wells
{\mathscr{E}}(m)
, and choose

r_{0}
small enough so that

\max_{m\in{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k_{m})}\sup_{y\in{\mathscr{E}}(m)}|u_{0}(y)-u_{0}(m)|\,\leq\,\delta\;.

By the previous estimate, for each
m\in M_{p+1}(x)
,
|s|\leq\vartheta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}
,

{\mathbb{E}}^{\epsilon}_{m}\big[u_{0}({\mathtt{x}}(\varrho_{\epsilon}-s))\,\big]\,=\,\sum_{m^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k_{m})}u_{0}(m^{\prime})\,{\mathbb{P}}^{\epsilon}_{m}\big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(\varrho_{\epsilon}-s)\in{\mathscr{E}}(m^{\prime})\,\big]\,+\,R^{(2)}_{\epsilon}(s)\,+\,R_{\delta}\;,

(8.9)

where
|R_{\delta}|\leq\delta
, and

|R^{(2)}_{\epsilon}(s)|\,\leq\,\|u_{0}\|_{\infty}\;{\mathbb{P}}^{\epsilon}_{m}\Big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(\varrho_{\epsilon}-s)\not\in{\mathscr{E}}({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k_{m}))\,\Big]\;.

We claim that

\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\sup_{|s|\leq\vartheta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}}{\mathbb{P}}^{\epsilon}_{m}\big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(\varrho_{\epsilon}-s)\not\in{\mathscr{E}}({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k_{m}))\,\big]\,=\,0\;.

(8.10)

Since

\varrho_{\epsilon}\succ\vartheta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}\succ\theta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}
, by (
7.5
) and Corollary
8.3
,

\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\sup_{|s|\leq\vartheta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}}{\mathbb{P}}^{\epsilon}_{m}\big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(\varrho_{\epsilon}-s)\not\in{\mathscr{E}}_{p}\,\big]\,=\,0\;,\quad\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\sup_{|s|\leq\vartheta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}}{\mathbb{P}}^{\epsilon}_{m}\big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(\varrho_{\epsilon}-s)\in{\mathscr{E}}_{p}\setminus{\mathscr{E}}_{p+1}\,\big]\,=\,0\;.

On the other hand, as

\varrho_{\epsilon}+\vartheta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}\prec\theta^{(p+1)}_{\epsilon}
, by Corollary
7.3
,

\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\sup_{|s|\leq\vartheta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}}{\mathbb{P}}^{\epsilon}_{m}\big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(\varrho_{\epsilon}-s)\in{\mathscr{E}}_{p+1}\setminus{\mathscr{E}}({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k_{m}))\,\big]\,=\,0\;,

which completes the proof of (
8.10
).

By Lemma
8.4
, the first term on the right-hand side of
(
8.9
) converges to

U_{p+1}(m)\,:=\,\sum_{m^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k_{m})}u_{0}(m^{\prime})\,\frac{\pi(m^{\prime})}{\pi({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k_{m}))}\;\cdot

In view of the previous
estimate, (
8.8
) is equal to

\sum_{m\in M_{p}(x)}U_{p+1}(m)\,{\mathbb{P}}^{\epsilon}_{x}\big[\,\tau({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1})<\vartheta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}\,,\,{\mathtt{x}}(\tau({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}))=m\,\big]\,+\,R^{(3)}_{\epsilon,\delta}(x)\;,

where

\limsup_{\epsilon\to 0}\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}}|\,R^{(3)}_{\epsilon,\delta}(x)\,|\,\leq\,(1+2\|u_{0}\|_{\infty})\,\delta\;.

By Corollary
7.4
we may remove the indicator of the event

\{\tau({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1})<\vartheta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}\}
. To complete the proof
of the theorem, it remains to apply Corollary
4.9
and to let

\delta\to 0
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
2.5
.(c)

The proof is identical to the one of part (b) provided we replace

\theta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}
by
\epsilon^{-1}
. There are only two
differences.

First, we apply Lemma
4.2
, instead of Corollary
7.4
, to
show that the term
R^{(1)}_{\epsilon}(x)
in (
8.8
) satisfies
(
7.3
). This explains why we need to assume that

\varrho_{\epsilon}\succ\epsilon^{-1}
.

Secondly, we replace Corollary
8.3
by the following result,
whose proof is analogous to the one of Corollary
8.3
.
Fix sequences
\varrho_{\epsilon}
,

\vartheta_{\epsilon}
such that

\epsilon^{-1}\prec\varrho_{\epsilon}
,

\vartheta_{\epsilon}\prec\varrho_{\epsilon}
. Then,

\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}}\sup_{|s|\leq\vartheta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}}{\mathbb{P}}^{\epsilon}_{x}\big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(\varrho_{\epsilon}-s)\in{\mathscr{E}}_{1}^{c}\,\big]\,=\,0\;.

The details are left to the reader.

∎

Proof of Theorem
2.8

The proof is similar to the one of Theorem
2.5
.(b).
Assume that the initial condition
u_{0}(\cdot)
is
\ell
-periodic.
Fix
\delta>0
, and recall from (
3.2
) the definition of the wells

{\mathscr{E}}(m)
. Since
u_{0}(\cdot)
is periodic, we may assume that

x\in[0,\ell)
, and choose
r_{0}
small enough so that

\max_{m\in{\mathcal{M}}}\sup_{y\in{\mathscr{E}}(m)}|u_{0}(y)-u_{0}(m)|\,\leq\,\delta\;.

(8.11)

Recall the definition of the Markov chain

{\mathbb{X}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},\ell}(\cdot)
, whose stationary state is
represented by
\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\ell}
. As the chain is
irreducible, for every
\delta>0
, there exists
t_{0}>0
such that

\max_{0\leq i,j<\ell\,{\mathfrak{u}}_{{\mathfrak{q}}}}\,\Big|\,{\mathtt{Q}}^{{\mathfrak{q}},\ell}_{{\mathscr{M}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},\ell}(i)}\big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(t_{0})={\mathscr{M}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},\ell}(j)\,\big]\,-\,\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\ell}({\mathscr{M}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},\ell}(j))\,\Big|\,\leq\,\delta\;,

where
{\mathtt{Q}}^{{\mathfrak{q}},\ell}_{{\mathscr{M}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},\ell}(i)}

represents the probability measure on the path space induced by the
Markov chain
{\mathbb{X}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},\ell}(\cdot)
starting
from
{\mathscr{M}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},\ell}(i)
.

As
{\mathtt{a}}(\cdot)
and
{\mathtt{b}}(\cdot)
are periodic, we may consider
that the diffusion
X_{\epsilon}(\cdot)
evolves on the torus

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathbb{T}}_{\ell}}:=[0,\ell)
. By the stochastic
representation (
8.7
) of the solution, by the Markov property,
and the
\ell
-translation invariance of
u_{0}(\cdot)
, as

\theta^{({\mathfrak{q}})}_{\epsilon}\prec\varrho_{\epsilon}
,

u(\varrho_{\epsilon},x)\;=\;{\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbb{T}}_{\ell},\epsilon}_{[x]_{\ell}}\Big[\,{\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbb{T}}_{\ell},\epsilon}_{{\mathtt{x}}(\varrho_{\epsilon}-t_{0}\theta^{({\mathfrak{q}})}_{\epsilon})}\big[\,u_{0}({\mathtt{x}}(t_{0}\theta^{({\mathfrak{q}})}_{\epsilon}))\,\big]\,\Big]\;,

where
[x]_{\ell}
is the representant of
x
in
{\mathbb{T}}_{\ell}
, and

\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathbb{P}}^{{\mathbb{T}}_{\ell},\epsilon}_{y}
,
y\in{\mathbb{T}}_{\ell}
, is
the probability measure on the path space induced by the diffusion
evolving on
{\mathbb{T}}_{\ell}
and starting from
y
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\Big|\,u_{\epsilon}(\varrho_{\epsilon},x)\,-\,\sum_{k=0}^{\ell{\mathfrak{u}}_{{\mathfrak{q}}}-1}\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\ell}({\mathscr{M}}_{{\mathfrak{q}}}(k)))\,\sum_{m\in{\mathscr{M}}_{{\mathfrak{q}}}(k)}\frac{\pi(m)}{\pi({\mathscr{M}}_{{\mathfrak{q}}}(k))}\,u_{0}(m)\,\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\;\sup_{y\in{\mathbb{T}}_{\ell}}\Big|\,{\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbb{T}}_{\ell},\epsilon}_{y}\big[\,u_{0}({\mathtt{x}}(t_{0}\theta^{({\mathfrak{q}})}_{\epsilon}))\,\big]\,-\,\sum_{k=0}^{\ell{\mathfrak{u}}_{{\mathfrak{q}}}-1}\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\ell}({\mathscr{M}}_{{\mathfrak{q}}}(k)))\,\sum_{m\in{\mathscr{M}}_{{\mathfrak{q}}}(k)}\frac{\pi(m)}{\pi({\mathscr{M}}_{{\mathfrak{q}}}(k))}\,u_{0}(m)\,\Big|\,.

We first move the starting point from
y\in{\mathbb{T}}_{\ell}
to

m\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathscr{M}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},\ell}}:=\cup_{j\in{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},\ell}}{\mathscr{M}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},\ell}(j)
. Fix a time-scale

\theta^{({\mathfrak{q}}-1)}_{\epsilon}\prec\vartheta_{\epsilon}\prec\theta^{({\mathfrak{q}})}_{\epsilon}
. Introduce the restriction

\{\tau({\mathscr{M}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},\ell})<\vartheta_{\epsilon}\}
, to obtain from
Corollary
7.4
that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbb{T}}_{\ell},\epsilon}_{y}\big[\,u_{0}({\mathtt{x}}(t_{0}\theta^{({\mathfrak{q}})}_{\epsilon}))\,\big]

(8.12)

\displaystyle\quad\,=\,\sum_{m\in{\mathscr{M}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},\ell}}{\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbb{T}}_{\ell},\epsilon}_{y}\Big[\,{\mathtt{1}}\big\{\tau({\mathscr{M}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},\ell})<\vartheta_{\epsilon}\,,\,{\mathtt{x}}(\tau({\mathscr{M}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},\ell}))=m\}\,{\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbb{T}}_{\ell},\epsilon}_{m}\big[u_{0}({\mathtt{x}}(t_{0}\theta^{({\mathfrak{q}})}_{\epsilon}-\tau({\mathscr{M}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},\ell})))\,\big]\,\Big]\,+\,R^{(1)}_{\epsilon}(y)\;,

where
R^{(1)}_{\epsilon}(y)
satisfies (
7.3
).

By (
8.11
), for all
0\leq s\leq\vartheta_{\epsilon}
,

m\in{\mathscr{M}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},\ell}
,

{\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbb{T}}_{\ell},\epsilon}_{m}\big[u_{0}({\mathtt{x}}(t_{0}\theta^{({\mathfrak{q}})}_{\epsilon}-s))\,\big]\,=\,\sum_{m^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{M}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},\ell}}u_{0}(m^{\prime})\,{\mathbb{P}}^{{\mathbb{T}}_{\ell},\epsilon}_{m}\big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(t_{0}\theta^{({\mathfrak{q}})}_{\epsilon}-s)\in{\mathscr{E}}(m^{\prime})\,\big]\,+\,R^{(2)}_{\epsilon}(s)\,+\,R_{\delta}\;,

(8.13)

where
|R_{\delta}|\leq\delta
, and

|R^{(2)}_{\epsilon}(s)|\,\leq\,\|u_{0}\|_{\infty}\;{\mathbb{P}}^{{\mathbb{T}}_{\ell},\epsilon}_{m}\Big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(t_{0}\theta^{(q)}_{\epsilon}-s)\not\in{\mathscr{E}}({\mathscr{M}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},\ell})\,\Big]\;.

By the proof of (
8.10
) with
p+1={\mathfrak{q}}
,
\varrho_{\epsilon}=\theta^{(q)}_{\epsilon}
,
\vartheta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}=\vartheta_{\epsilon}
,
since
\vartheta_{\epsilon}\succ\theta^{({\mathfrak{q}}-1)}_{\epsilon}

\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\sup_{|s|\leq\vartheta_{\epsilon}}|R^{(2)}_{\epsilon}(s)|\,=\,0\;.

(8.14)

Mind that we do not need the last part of the argument, where
Corollary
7.3
is used, because
{\mathscr{M}}_{{\mathfrak{q}},\ell}

contains all the wells of the last layer.

It remains to estimate the probability appearing in (
8.13
).
Suppose that
m\in{\mathscr{M}}_{{\mathfrak{q}}}(j)
,
m^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{M}}_{{\mathfrak{q}}}(k)
for
some
0\leq j,k<\ell\,{\mathfrak{u}}_{{\mathfrak{q}}}
. We claim that

\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\sup_{|s|\leq\vartheta_{\epsilon}}\Big|\,{\mathbb{P}}_{m}^{{\mathbb{T}}_{\ell},\epsilon}\big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(t_{0}\theta^{(q)}_{\epsilon}-s)\in{\mathscr{E}}(m^{\prime})\,\big]\,-\,\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\ell}({\mathscr{M}}_{{\mathfrak{q}}}(k)))\,\,\frac{\pi(m^{\prime})}{\pi({\mathscr{M}}_{{\mathfrak{q}}}(k))}\,\Big|\,=\,0\;.

Bound this expression by

\displaystyle\Big|\,{\mathbb{P}}_{m}^{{\mathbb{T}}_{\ell},\epsilon}\big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(t_{0}\theta^{(q)}_{\epsilon}-s)\in{\mathscr{E}}(m^{\prime})\,\big]\,-\,{\mathtt{Q}}^{\ell,{\mathfrak{q}}}_{{\mathscr{M}}_{{\mathfrak{q}}}(j)}\big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(t_{0})={\mathscr{M}}_{{\mathfrak{q}}}(k)\,]\,\frac{\pi(m^{\prime})}{\pi({\mathscr{M}}_{{\mathfrak{q}}}(k))}\,\Big|

\displaystyle+\,\Big|\,{\mathtt{Q}}^{\ell,{\mathfrak{q}}}_{{\mathscr{M}}_{{\mathfrak{q}}}(j)}\big[\,{\mathtt{x}}(t_{0})={\mathscr{M}}_{{\mathfrak{q}}}(k)\,]\,\frac{\pi(m^{\prime})}{\pi({\mathscr{M}}_{{\mathfrak{q}}}(k))}\,-\,\pi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\ell}({\mathscr{M}}_{{\mathfrak{q}}}(k)))\,\frac{\pi(m^{\prime})}{\pi({\mathscr{M}}_{{\mathfrak{q}}}(k))}\,\Big|

By the choice of
t_{0}
, the second term is bounded by
\delta
.
By Lemma
8.2
, the first one vanishes as
\epsilon\to 0
. This
completes the proof of the theorem.

Appendix A
The hierarchical structure

In this section, we prove the assertions of Section
2

regarding the construction of the hierarchy structure of the model,
and present further properties of this structure needed in the proof
of Theorems
3.2
,
3.3
.

Proof of Property
{\mathcal{P}}_{10}(1)
.

By postulate
{\mathcal{P}}_{9}(1)
, the jump rates
R_{1}
are
N
-periodic.
There exists, therefore,
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
such that
{\mathfrak{h}}_{1}=h^{1}_{k}
.
Since
\pi_{1}(k)\,\sigma_{1}(k,k\pm 1)
,
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, is strictly
positive and finite,

R_{1}({\mathscr{M}}_{1}(k),{\mathscr{M}}_{1}(k+1))+R_{1}({\mathscr{M}}_{1}(k),{\mathscr{M}}_{1}(k-1))>0
. The rest of the proof is analogous to the one of

{\mathcal{P}}_{10}(p+1)
presented in the proof of Proposition
2.4

below.
∎

Proof of Proposition
2.2
.

Property
{\mathcal{P}}_{1}(p+1)
,
{\mathcal{P}}_{2}(p+1)
have been proved right before
the statement of the proposition. We turn to postulate

{\mathcal{P}}_{3}(p+1)
. By (
2.15
), the definition of the sets

{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(j)
,
j\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, and condition
{\mathcal{P}}_{2}(p+1)
,

1+{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(0)={\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k)
for some
k\geq 0
. The index

k
cannot be
0
because we have already shown that the sets

{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(j)
are bounded. [If

1+{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(0)={\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(0)
, then

1+m_{{\textsf{j}}_{p+1}}\in 1+{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(0)={\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(0)
, and by
induction
j+m_{{\textsf{j}}_{p+1}}\in{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(0)
for all
j\geq 1
].

By property
{\mathcal{P}}_{2}(p+1)
,
{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(0)<{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(1)<\cdots<{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k-1)<{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k)=1+{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(0)
. Thus
k
represents the number of equivalent classes in
{\mathscr{S}}_{p+1}
, which has been denoted by
{\mathfrak{u}}_{p+1}
. This proves
property
{\mathcal{P}}_{3}(p+1)
.

Consider postulate
{\mathcal{P}}_{4}(p+1)
. Fix
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. By
construction,

{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k)=\cup_{j:{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(j)\in{\mathfrak{R}}}{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(j)
for
some
{\mathbb{X}}_{p}
-recurrent class
{\mathfrak{R}}
. By postulate
{\mathcal{P}}_{4}(p)
,
all elements of
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(j)
have the same depth. It remains to show
that
S({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(j))
is constant.

By postulate
{\mathcal{P}}_{7}(p)
,
{\mathbb{X}}_{p}(\cdot)
jumps only to nearest
neighbour sets, and by condition
{\mathcal{P}}_{2}(p)
, the sets
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(j)

are ordered. Thus, as
{\mathbb{X}}^{p}(\cdot)
has more than one recurrent
class,
{\mathfrak{R}}=\{{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(j):a\leq j\leq b\}
for some

-\infty<a\leq b<+\infty
. Denote
a
,
b
by
l_{p+1}(k)
,

r_{p+1}(k)
, respectively, so that

{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k)\,:=\,\bigcup_{i=l_{p+1}(k)}^{r_{p+1}(k)}{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(j)\;.

(A.1)

Moreover,
R_{p}({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(i),{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(i+1))>0
,

R_{p}({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(i+1),{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(i))>0
for all

l_{p+1}(k)\leq i<r_{p+1}(k)
, Thus, by postulate
{\mathcal{P}}_{8}(p)
,

S({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(j))=S({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(l_{p+1}(k)))
for all

l_{p+1}(k)\leq j\leq r_{p+1}(k)
, what proves postulate
{\mathcal{P}}_{4}(p+1)
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
2.3
.

We first show that
{\mathfrak{h}}_{p+1}>{\mathfrak{h}}_{p}
. By the definition
(
2.16
)–(
2.17
) of
{\mathfrak{h}}_{p+1}
, to prove this inequality,
we have to show that

\Lambda\,\big({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k+1)\,,\,{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k)\big)\,-\,S({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k+i))\,>\,{\mathfrak{h}}_{p}

(A.2)

for all
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,
i=0
,
1
.

We prove (
A.2
) for
i=1
. The same argument applies to
i=0
. Fix

k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. Recall the notation introduced in (
A.1
). By
definition, the set
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(r_{p+1}(k)+1)
does not belong to the

{\mathbb{X}}^{p}(\cdot)
-recurrent class of
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(r_{p+1}(k))
, so that

R^{p}({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(r_{p+1}(k)),{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(r_{p+1}(k)+1))=0
. Hence, by
conditions
{\mathcal{P}}_{6}(p)
,
{\mathcal{P}}_{7}(p)
,

\Lambda\,\big({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(r_{p+1}(k))\,,\,{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(r_{p+1}(k)+1)\big)\,-\,S({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(r_{p+1}(k)))\,>\,{\mathfrak{h}}_{p}\;.

Since
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(r_{p+1}(k))\,\subset\,{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k)
, by definition
of
{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k)
and postulate
{\mathcal{P}}_{4}(p+1)
,

S({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(r_{p+1}(k)))=S({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k))
. On the other
hand, by definition of
l_{p+1}(k+1)
,
r_{p+1}(k)+1\leq l_{p+1}(k+1)
so that

\Lambda({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(r_{p+1}(k))\,,\,{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(r_{p+1}(k)+1))\,\leq\,\Lambda\,({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(r_{p+1}(k))\,,\,{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(l_{p+1}(k+1)))\;.

By definition of
r_{p+1}(k)
,
l_{p+1}(k+1)
, the right-hand side is
equal to
\Lambda\,({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k)\,,\,{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k+1))
, which
completes the proof of (
A.2
).

We turn to the proof of postulate
{\mathcal{P}}_{5}(p+1)
. Fix

k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, and assume that
{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k)
has two or more
elements. Let
m^{\prime}
,
m^{\prime\prime}\in{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k)
. Suppose, first, that

m^{\prime}
,
m^{\prime\prime}
belong to the same set

{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(i)\subset{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k)
. By postulates
{\mathcal{P}}_{5}(p)
,

{\mathcal{P}}_{4}(p+1)
and since
{\mathfrak{h}}_{p}<{\mathfrak{h}}_{p+1}
,

\Lambda(m^{\prime},m^{\prime\prime})\leq S({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(i))+{\mathfrak{h}}_{p-1}\,=\,S({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k))+{\mathfrak{h}}_{p-1}<S({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k))+{\mathfrak{h}}_{p}\;.

Recall the notation introduced in (
A.1
), and suppose now that

m^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(i)
,
m^{\prime\prime}\in{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(j)
for some
i
,

j\in\{l_{p+1}(k),\dots,r_{p+1}(k)\}
,
i<j
. Recall from
(
2.19
) the definition of the rightmost, leftmost,
m^{+}_{p,i}
,

m^{-}_{p,i}
, element of
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(i)
, respectively. Then,

\Lambda(m^{\prime},m^{\prime\prime})\,\leq\,\max\Big\{\Lambda(m^{\prime},m^{+}_{p,i})\,,\,\Lambda(m^{+}_{p,i},m^{-}_{p,i+1})\,,\,\dots\,,\,\Lambda(m^{+}_{p,j-1},m^{-}_{p,j})\,,\,\Lambda(m^{-}_{p,j},m^{\prime\prime})\,\Big\}\;.

As above, by postulates
{\mathcal{P}}_{5}(p)
,
{\mathcal{P}}_{4}(p+1)
, and since
{\mathfrak{h}}_{p}<{\mathfrak{h}}_{p+1}
,

\Lambda(m^{-}_{p,o},m^{+}_{p,o})\,\leq\,S({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(o))\,+\,{\mathfrak{h}}_{p-1}\,=\,S({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k))\,+\,{\mathfrak{h}}_{p-1}

for all
i<o<j
. The same argument permits to bound the first and last
term on the right-hand side of the penultimate displayed formula by

S({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k))+{\mathfrak{h}}_{p}
. On the other hand, as
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(o)
,

{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(o+1)
belong to the same
{\mathbb{X}}^{p}(\cdot)
-recurrent class, by
definition of
m^{+}_{p,o}

m^{-}_{p,o+1}
, properties
{\mathcal{P}}_{7}(p)
,

{\mathcal{P}}_{4}(p+1)

\Lambda(m^{+}_{p,o},m^{-}_{p,o+1})\,=\,\Lambda({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(o),{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(o+1))\,=\,S({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(o))\,+\,{\mathfrak{h}}_{p}\,=\,S({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k))\,+\,{\mathfrak{h}}_{p}\;.

This completes the proof of postulate
{\mathcal{P}}_{5}(p+1)
.

Postulate
{\mathcal{P}}_{6}(p+1)
follows from the definition (
2.17
) of

{\mathfrak{h}}_{p+1}
. This completes the proof of the proposition;
∎

Proof of Proposition
2.4
.

Condition
{\mathcal{P}}_{7}(p+1)
follows from the definition (
2.22
) of
the jump rates. We turn to
{\mathcal{P}}_{8}(p+1)
. Fix
j\in{\mathbb{Z}}
and
suppose that
R_{q}({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j),{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j+1))>0
. The same argument
applies to
j-1
. By definition of the rate function,

h^{p+1,+}_{j}={\mathfrak{h}}_{p+1}
so that, by (
2.16
),

\Lambda\,\big({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(j+1)\,,\,{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(j)\big)-S({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(j))\,=\,{\mathfrak{h}}_{p+1}\;.

By
{\mathcal{P}}_{6}(p+1)
(for
k=j+1
), this quantity is bounded above by

h^{p+1,-}_{j+1}\,=\,\Lambda\,\big({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(j+1)\,,\,{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(j)\big)-S({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(j+1))\;,

so that
S({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(j+1))\leq S({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(j))
, as required. If

R_{q}({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j+1),{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j))=0
, then, by definition of the jump
rates,
h^{p+1,-}_{j+1}>{\mathfrak{h}}_{p+1}
. Thus, the penultimate
displayed equation is strictly less than the last one, and

S({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(j+1))<S({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(j))
. This completes
the proof of postulate
{\mathcal{P}}_{8}(p+1)
.

Consider postulate
{\mathcal{P}}_{9}(p+1)
. By the
1
-periodicity of
the functions
{\textsf{a}}(\cdot)
,
{\textsf{b}}(\cdot)
, and postulate

{\mathcal{P}}_{3}(p+1)
,
\pi_{p+1}(k+{\mathfrak{u}}_{p+1})=\pi_{p+1}(k)
,

\sigma_{p+1}(k+{\mathfrak{u}}_{p+1},k+1+{\mathfrak{u}}_{p+1})=\sigma_{p+1}(k,k+1)

for all
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. Hence, by (
2.18
), postulate
{\mathcal{P}}_{9}(p+1)

is in force.

To prove claim (
2.24
), note that the weights
\pi_{p+1}(j)
,

\sigma_{p+1}(j,j+1)
,
j\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, are strictly positive. Hence,
(
2.24
) follows from the fact, mentioned just before the statement
of Proposition
2.3
, that
{\mathfrak{h}}^{p+1}=h^{p+1}_{k}
for some

k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.

We complete the proof of the proposition showing that

{\mathfrak{u}}_{p+2}<{\mathfrak{u}}_{p+1}
, which is postulate
{\mathcal{P}}_{10}(p+1)
.
Fix
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
for which (
2.24
) holds. By postulate

{\mathcal{P}}_{9}(p+1)
, there exists
0\leq k^{\prime}<{\mathfrak{u}}_{p+1}
for which
(
2.24
) holds. Since one of the jump rates is strictly positive,

{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k^{\prime})
is either a transient state or belongs to a
recurrent class with at least two states. In both cases

{\mathfrak{u}}_{p+2}<{\mathfrak{u}}_{p+1}
.

More precisely. Suppose, first, that
{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k^{\prime})
is a transient
state. Then, by the definition (
2.13
), the number of sets

{\mathscr{M}}^{*}_{p+2}(j)
containing one of the sets
{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(i)
,

0\leq i<{\mathfrak{u}}_{p+1}
, is at most
{\mathfrak{u}}_{p+1}-1
, because

{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k^{\prime})
is a transient state. Thus,

{\mathfrak{u}}_{p+2}<{\mathfrak{u}}_{p+1}
.

Suppose, next, that
{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k^{\prime})
belongs to a recurrent class,
and assume without loss of generality that

R_{p+1}({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k^{\prime}),{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k^{\prime}-1))>0
. Since

{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k^{\prime})
belongs to a recurrent class,

R_{p+1}({\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k^{\prime}+1),{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k^{\prime}))>0
also. Thus,

{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k^{\prime})
and
{\mathscr{M}}_{p+1}(k^{\prime}-1)
are both contained in the
same set
{\mathscr{M}}^{*}_{p+2}(j)
so that
{\mathfrak{u}}_{p+2}<{\mathfrak{u}}_{p+1}
.
∎

Further properties of the metastable structure

We present in this subsection additional properties of the higher order
metastable structure. We start with simple observations. Let

{\mathscr{T}}_{1}=\varnothing
, and define recursively
{\mathscr{T}}_{p+1}
as

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathscr{T}}_{p+1}}\,:=\,{\mathscr{T}}_{p}\,\cup\,\bigcup_{i\colon{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(i)\in{\mathfrak{T}}_{p}}{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(i)\,,\;1\leq p\leq{\mathfrak{q}}\,,\;\;\text{so that}\;\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathscr{P}}_{q}}:=\{{\mathscr{M}}_{q},{\mathscr{T}}_{q}\}\,,\;1\leq q\leq{\mathfrak{q}}+1\,,

(A.3)

is a partition of
{\mathcal{M}}
.

Lemma A.1
.

For
1\leq p\leq{\mathfrak{q}}
,
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)\subset(0,1)
,
1\leq k<{\mathfrak{u}}_{p}
. In particular, by
{\mathcal{P}}_{2}(p)
,

{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(-1)<0<{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(1)<\cdots<{\mathscr{M}}_{p}({\mathfrak{u}}_{p}-1)<1\;,\quad\text{and}\;\;{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(0)\cap(0,1)\,\neq\,\varnothing\;.

(A.4)

Proof.

Since, by
{\mathcal{P}}_{2}(p)
,
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(0)
contains

m_{{\textsf{j}}_{p}}\in(0,1)
, and the sets
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(j)
are ordered,
it is enough to show that
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}({\mathfrak{u}}_{p}-1)\subset(0,1)
. To
prove this relation, observe that, by
{\mathcal{P}}_{2}(p)
,
{\mathcal{P}}_{3}(p)
,

{\mathscr{M}}_{p}({\mathfrak{u}}_{p}-1)<{\mathscr{M}}_{p}({\mathfrak{u}}_{p})=1+{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(0)
. Thus,
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}({\mathfrak{u}}_{p}-1)<1+m_{{\textsf{j}}_{p}}
. By
definition of
{\textsf{j}}_{p}
,
m_{i}\not\in{\mathscr{M}}_{p}
for

0\leq i<{\textsf{j}}_{p}
. Hence, by
{\mathcal{P}}_{3}(p)
,

1+m_{i}\not\in{\mathscr{M}}_{p}
for
0\leq i<{\textsf{j}}_{p}
. We may thus
improve the upper bound of
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}({\mathfrak{u}}_{p}-1)
and get that

{\mathscr{M}}_{p}({\mathfrak{u}}_{p}-1)<1+m_{0}
because
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}({\mathfrak{u}}_{p}-1)
only
contains
S
-local minima. Since there is no
S
-local minima in the
interval
[1,1+m_{0})
,
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}({\mathfrak{u}}_{p}-1)<1
.
∎

Fix
2\leq p\leq{\mathfrak{q}}
and
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)
,
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. The set

{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)
is composed of local minima of
S(\cdot)
.
There might be other local minima of
S
between two elements of

{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)
. The first assertion of this section states
that these local minima are “higher” than the ones of

{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)
, which, according to postulate
{\mathcal{P}}_{4}(p)
are
all at the same height. A precise statement requires some notation.

We refer to Figure
3
for an illustration of the next
definitions. Recall from (
2.19
) the definitions of the leftmost

m_{p,k}^{-}
and rightmost
m_{p,k}^{+}
elements of
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)
,
respectively. Keep in mind that
m_{p,k}^{-}
,
m_{p,k}^{+}
might
coincide if
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)
is a singleton.

Recall from (
2.20
) the definition of the set

{\mathscr{W}}^{(p)}_{j,j+1}
,
j\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. Let
\sigma^{p,+}_{j,j+1}
,

\sigma^{p,-}_{j,j+1}
be the rightmost and leftmost maxima in

{\mathscr{W}}^{(p)}_{j,j+1}
, respectively:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sigma^{p,+}_{j,j+1}}\,:=\,\max\{\sigma\in{\mathscr{W}}^{(p)}_{j,j+1}\}\;,\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sigma^{p,-}_{j,j+1}}\,:=\,\min\{\sigma\in{\mathscr{W}}^{(p)}_{j,j+1}\}\;.

(A.5)

Recall from (
1.1
) that
{\mathcal{M}}\subset{\mathbb{R}}
represents the set of
local minima of
S(\cdot)
. Denote by
{\textsf{M}}_{p}(k)
the set of local
minima of
S(\cdot)
which belong to the interval

[\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k},\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1}]
and are not in

{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)
:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\textsf{M}}_{p}(k)}\,:=\,\big(\,{\mathcal{M}}\cap[\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k},\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1}]\,\big)\setminus{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)\;.

As illustrated in Figure
3
, some elements of
{\textsf{M}}_{p}(k)

may belong to the interval
[\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k},m^{-}_{p,k})
or to

[m^{+}_{p,k},\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1})
.

\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1}
\sigma^{p,+}_{k,k+1}
m^{p,-}_{k}
m^{p,+}_{k}
\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k}

Figure 3.
Here we illustrate some of the important definitions
surrounding the structure of a higher order metastable state. Fixed

p>1
and
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, the state
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)
is represented in the
figure by the cyan circles, while the set
M_{p}(k)
is composed by the
green circles. The orange circles represent the local minima in
between the left-most and right-most maxima separating the states

{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)
and
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k+1)
. As stated in Proposition
A.2
,
the minima
contained in
M_{p}(k)
are ”higher” than the ones contained in

{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)
, which are all of the same height. Regarding the escape
barriers of the well
{\mathscr{E}}({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k))
, while we have only one
global maxima separating
{\mathscr{E}}({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k))
and

{\mathscr{E}}({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k-1))
, there are three between
{\mathscr{E}}({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k))
and

{\mathscr{E}}({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k+1))
. Here,

\sigma_{k-1,k}^{p,+}=\sigma_{k-1,k}^{p,-}
, while

\sigma_{k,k+1}^{p,-}\,<\,\sigma_{k,k+1}^{p,+}
.

Proposition A.2
.

For all
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,

\min\{S(m):m\in{\textsf{M}}_{p}(k)\,\}\,>\,S({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k))\;.

We assume below that
{\textsf{M}}_{p}(k)
is not empty, otherwise there is
nothing to prove. The proof of this result requires some elementary
properties of the construction.

Lemma A.3
.

For each
2\leq p\leq{\mathfrak{q}}
,
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,
m\in{\textsf{M}}_{p}(k)
, there
exist
1\leq q<p
,
j\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, such that
m\in{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)
and

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)
is a
{\mathbb{X}}_{q}
-transient set. With the notation
introduced in (
A.3
),
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)\in{\mathfrak{T}}_{q}
.

Proof.

Fix
m\in{\textsf{M}}_{p}(k)
. By postulate
{\mathscr{P}}_{2}(p)
,
m
does not belong
to any of the sets
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(j)
,
j\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. [By definition it does
not belong to
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)
. By postulate
{\mathscr{P}}_{2}(p)
the sets

{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(j)
are ordered. By definition it is to the right of

{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k-1)
and to the left of
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k+1)
.] Hence, by
(
A.3
),
m\in{\mathscr{T}}_{p}
, and there exist
1\leq q<p
,

j\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, such that
m\in{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)\in{\mathfrak{T}}_{q}
, as
claimed.
∎

For
m\in{\textsf{M}}_{p}(k)
, denote by
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p_{m}
the index

1\leq q<p
given by Lemma
A.3
.

Remark A.4
.

By construction, for each
1\leq q<p
,
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, there exists at
least one
\ell\in{\mathbb{Z}}
such that
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(\ell)
is

{\mathbb{X}}_{q}
-recurrent and
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(\ell)\subset{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)
.

Suppose that there exist
1\leq q<p
,
j\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, such that

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)\cap(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k},\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1})\neq\varnothing
, and
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)
is
{\mathbb{X}}_{q}
-transient. By
construction,
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)\cap{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)=\varnothing
. In this
case, by the previous paragraph, there exist at least two sets,

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)
and
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(\ell)
, whose intersection with the
interval
(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k},\sigma^{p,-}_{k.k+1})
is not
empty. Corollary
A.6
asserts that
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)
is actually
contained in the interval

(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k},\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1})
.

Recall that
{\mathcal{W}}\subset{\mathbb{R}}
represents the set of local maxima of

S(\cdot)
. Next result states that the energetic barriers at

\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k}
,
\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1}
are higher than the ones in
the interior of the interval

(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k}\,,\,\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1})
. It follows from this
result that the diffusion
X_{\epsilon}(t)
is trapped in the interval

(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k}\,,\,\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1})
in time-scales of smaller
order than
\theta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}
.

Lemma A.5
.

For each
2\leq p\leq{\mathfrak{q}}
,
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,

\displaystyle S(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k})\,>\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}S^{-}_{p,k}}\>=\,\max\{\,S(x):x\in(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k},m^{+}_{p,k}]\cap{\mathcal{W}}\,\big\}\;,

\displaystyle S(\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1})\,>\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}S^{+}_{p,k}}\,:=\,\max\{S(x):x\in[m^{-}_{p,k},\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1})\cap{\mathcal{W}}\}\;.

Proof.

We prove the first bound. The arguments extend to the second.
By definition of
\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k}
,

\displaystyle S(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k})\,>\,\max\{S(x):x\in(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k},m^{-}_{p,k}]\cap{\mathcal{W}}\}\;,

This strict inequality can be extended to the local maxima of

S(\cdot)
in the interval
[m^{-}_{p,k},m^{+}_{p,k}]
. Indeed, suppose
that
m^{-}_{p,k}<m^{+}_{p,k}
, and fix a local maximum
\sigma
in this
interval. By postulate
{\mathcal{P}}_{5}(p)
, and Proposition
2.3
,

S(\sigma)-S({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k))\leq\Lambda(m^{-}_{p,k},m^{+}_{p,k})-S({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k))\leq{\mathfrak{h}}_{p-1}<{\mathfrak{h}}_{p}
. On the other hand, by condition

{\mathcal{P}}_{6}(p)
, and since

S(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k})=\Lambda({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k-1),{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k))
,

S(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k})\,-\,S({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k))\,\geq\,{\mathfrak{h}}_{p}\;.

(A.6)

Hence,
S(\sigma)<S(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k})
, which completes the
proof of the lemma.
∎

Corollary A.6
.

Suppose that

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)\cap(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k}\,,\,\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1})\neq\varnothing
for some
j\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,
1\leq q<p
. Then,

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)\subset(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k}\,,\,\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1})
.

Proof.

Fix
m\in{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)\cap(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k}\,,\,\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1})
for some
j\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,
1\leq q<p
.

By construction, a set
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(\ell)
,
\ell\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, is either
contained in
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)
or in its complement. Thus, if

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)\cap{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)\neq\varnothing
, there is noting to
prove because

{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)\subset(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k}\,,\,\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1})
.

Assume therefore that
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)\cap{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)=\varnothing
,
and that
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)
is not a singleton. Let
m^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)
,

m^{\prime}\neq m
, say
m^{\prime}<m
. By construction, there exists
\ell\in{\mathbb{Z}}

such that
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(\ell)\subset{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)
. Let

m_{*}\in{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(\ell)
. Since, by postulate
{\mathcal{P}}_{2}(q)
, the sets

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(i)
are ordered, either
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)<{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(\ell)
or

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(\ell)<{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)
. If

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(\ell)<{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)
, there is nothing to prove because

\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k}<{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(\ell)<m^{\prime}<m<\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1}
. Assume, therefore, that

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)<{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(\ell)
.

Since
m^{\prime}<m
, assume by contradiction that
m^{\prime}<\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k}
so
that
\Lambda(m,m^{\prime})\geq S(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k})
. On the other hand, by

{\mathcal{P}}_{5}(q)
and Proposition
2.3
,

\Lambda(m,m^{\prime})<S({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j))+{\mathfrak{h}}_{q}
, so that

S(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k})<S({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j))+{\mathfrak{h}}_{q}
.

Since
m\in{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)
,
m_{*}\in{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(\ell)
, and

\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k}<m<m_{*}<m^{+}_{p,k}
[because

m^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)
,
m_{*}\in{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(\ell)\subset{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)
,

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)<{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(\ell)
], by (
2.11
), and the definition
of
S^{-}_{p,k}
,

\Lambda({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j),{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(\ell))\leq\Lambda(m,m_{*})\leq S^{-}_{p,k}
.

On the other hand, as
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)<{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(\ell)
, by postulates

{\mathcal{P}}_{2}(q)
,
{\mathcal{P}}_{6}(q)
, and the definition of the barrier

\Lambda(\cdot,\cdot)
,

\Lambda({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j),{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(\ell))-S({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j))\geq\Lambda({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j),{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j+1))-S({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j))\geq{\mathfrak{h}}_{q}
.

Putting together the previous estimates yields that

S(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k})\,<\,S({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j))+{\mathfrak{h}}_{q}\,\leq\,\Lambda({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j),{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(\ell))\,\leq\,S^{-}_{p,k}\;,

which contradicts the assertion of the previous lemma.
∎

Fix
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)
for some
1\leq q<p
,
j\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, such that

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)\subset(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k},\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1})
,

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)\cap{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)=\varnothing
. Let

\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j_{+})
,
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j_{-})
the
rightmost, leftmost set which can be attained by the Markov chain

{\mathbb{X}}_{q}(\cdot)
starting from
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)
, respectively. Thus,

j_{+}=j
if
R^{q}({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j),{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j+1))=0
, and

j_{+}:=\max\{r\geq j:R^{q}({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(i-1),{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(i))>0\;,\;\;j<i\leq r\}
, otherwise. Similarly,
j_{-}=j
if

R^{q}({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j),{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j-1))=0
, and

j_{-}:=\min\{r\geq j:R^{q}({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(i+1),{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(i))>0\;,\;\;r\leq i<j\}
. Since
q<p\leq{\mathfrak{q}}
,
{\mathbb{X}}_{q}(\cdot)
has at least two
closed irreducible classes, up to equivalences, so that
j_{\pm}
are
well defined and finite.

Lemma A.7
.

Fix
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)
for some
1\leq q<p
,

j\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, such that

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)\subset(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k},\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1})
,

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)\cap{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)=\varnothing
. Then, with the notation introduced above,

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j_{-})\cup{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j_{+})\,\subset\,(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k},\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1})\;.

Proof.

We prove the result for
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j_{-})
, as the
same argument applies to
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j_{+})
.

Let
\ell\in{\mathbb{Z}}
such that
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(\ell)\subset{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)

given by Remark
A.4
. By postulate
{\mathcal{P}}_{2}(q)
, the sets

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(r)
,
r\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, are ordered. Assume that

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)<{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(\ell)
. The same argument applies for the
other case.

As
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)<{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(\ell)
and

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(\ell)\subset{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)\subset(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k},\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1})
,

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j_{-})<\{\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1}\}
. It remains
to show that

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j_{-})>\{\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k}\}
.

We claim that

{\mathfrak{h}}_{q}\,\leq\,S^{-}_{p,k}-S({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j))\;.

(A.7)

Indeed, by postulate
{\mathcal{P}}_{6}(q)
,

{\mathfrak{h}}_{q}\leq\Lambda({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j),{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j+1))-S({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j))
. Since
\ell>j
, by the definition of the barrier height

\Lambda(\cdot,\cdot)
, the previous expression is less than or
equal to
\Lambda({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j),{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(\ell))-S({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j))
. Since, by hypothesis, the sets
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)
,

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(\ell)
are contained in the interval

(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k},m^{+}_{p,k}]
, by the definition of
S^{-}_{p,k}
,
the previous expression is bounded by
S^{-}_{p,k}-S({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j))
. To
complete the proof of (
A.7
), it remains to recollect the previous
estimates.

We are now in a position to show that

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j_{-})>\{\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k}\}
.
Suppose, by contradiction, that

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j_{-})<\{\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k}\}
. In
particular,
j_{-}<j
.

As
R^{q}({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(r+1),{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(r))>0
, for
j_{-}\leq r<j
, by postulate

{\mathcal{P}}_{8}(q)
,
S({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j-n))
is a decreasing sequence for

0\leq n\leq j-j_{-}
. Hence
S({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(r))\leq S({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j))
for

j_{-}\leq r\leq j
.

Recall the assertion of Corollary
A.6
, and denote by
j_{0}
the
index such that

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j_{0})<\{\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k}\}<{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j_{0}+1)
. As

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j_{-})<\{\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k}\}
,
j_{0}\geq j_{-}
. In
particular,
R^{q}({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j_{0}+1),{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j_{0})>0
. Thus, by postulate

{\mathcal{P}}_{7}(q)
,

{\mathfrak{h}}_{k}=\Lambda({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j_{0}),{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j_{0}+1))-S({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j_{0}+1))
. By definition of
j_{0}
, this quantity is larger than
or equal to
S(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k})-S({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j_{0}+1))
. Since

S({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j-n))
is a decreasing sequence, and
j_{0}+1\leq j
[by
definition of
j_{0}
and because

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)\subset(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k},\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1})]
,
this quantity is bounded below by

S(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k})-S({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j))
. Adding together the
previous estimates yields that

{\mathfrak{h}}_{k}\geq S(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k})-S({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j))
.

By the previous inequality and (
A.7
),

S(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k})\leq S^{-}_{p,k}
, in contradiction with Lemma

A.5
. This shows that

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j_{-})>\{\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k}\}
, and
completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

In the next result, we keep the same notation of Lemma
A.7
, and
we further assume that
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)
is
{\mathbb{X}}_{q}
-transient.

Lemma A.8
.

Fix
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)
for some
1\leq q<p
,

j\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, such that

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)\subset(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k},\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1})
,

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)\cap{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)=\varnothing
. Assume that
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)
is
{\mathbb{X}}_{q}
-transient.
Then, there exists

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathfrak{s}}(j)}\in\{j_{-},\dots,j_{+}\}
such that

S({\mathscr{M}}_{q}({\mathfrak{s}}(j)))<S({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j))
,

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}({\mathfrak{s}}(j))\subset(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k},\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1})
.

Proof.

Recall the notation introduced above the statement of Lemma
A.7
.
Let
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(k_{+})
,
k_{+}\geq j
, be the rightmost set
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(r)

which can attain
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)
with left jumps:
k_{+}=j
if

R^{q}({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j+1),{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j))=0
, and

k_{+}:=\max\{r\geq j:R^{q}({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(i),{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(i-1))>0\;,\;\;j<i\leq r\}
, otherwise. Define
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(k_{-})
,
k_{-}\leq j
,
similarly, as the leftmost set
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(r)
which can attain

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)
with right jumps. Since
q<p\leq{\mathfrak{q}}
,
{\mathbb{X}}_{q}
has
at least two closed irreducible class, up to equivalences, so that

k_{\pm}
are finite and well defined. We do not claim that the sets

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(k_{-})
,
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(k_{+})
are contained in the interval

(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k},\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1})
.

Suppose that
k_{-}\leq j_{-}
and
k_{+}\geq j_{+}
. In this case,

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)
belongs to a
{\mathbb{X}}_{q}
-closed irreducible class, in
contradiction with the initial assumption that it is

{\mathbb{X}}_{q}
-transient. Hence,
k_{-}>j_{-}
or
k_{+}<j_{+}
.

Assume, without loss of generality, that
k_{+}<j_{+}
. Thus, the Markov
chain
{\mathbb{X}}_{q}
may jump back and forth between
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(i)
and

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(i+1)
for
j\leq i<k_{+}
, it may jump from
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(k_{+})

to
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(k_{+}+1)
, and it can not jump from
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(k_{+}+1)
to

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(k_{+})
. By postulate
{\mathcal{P}}_{8}(q)
,

S({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j))=S({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j+1))=\cdots=S({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(k_{+}))>S({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(k_{+}+1))
. By Lemma
A.7
, since
k_{+}<j_{+}
,

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(k_{+}+1)\subset(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k},\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1})
. Thus,
{\mathfrak{s}}(j)=k_{+}+1
fullfils all
requisites of the lemma.
∎

Recall from Lemma
A.3
that we denote by
p_{m}
,

m\in{\textsf{M}}_{p}(k)
, the index of
m
.

Proof of Proposition
A.2
.

Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}q_{1}}:=\min\{p_{m}:m\in{\textsf{M}}_{p}(k)\}
,

q_{i+1}:=\min\{p_{m}>q_{i}:m\in{\textsf{M}}_{p}(k)\}
,
i\geq 1
. Since

q_{i}
is an increasing sequence bounded by
p
, it has only a finite
number of terms,
q_{1}<\cdots<q_{i_{0}}<p
. The proof is by backward
induction on
q_{i}
: we prove that the result holds for

m\in{\textsf{M}}_{p}(k)
such that
p_{m}=q_{i_{0}}
; and that it holds for

m\in{\textsf{M}}_{p}(k)
such that
p_{m}=q_{i}
if it holds for

m^{\prime}\in{\textsf{M}}_{p}(k)
such that
p_{m^{\prime}}=q_{j}
,
i<j\leq i_{0}
.

Fix
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}q=q_{i}
for some
1\leq i\leq i_{0}
, and

m\in{\textsf{M}}_{p}(k)
such that
p_{m}=q
. As
p_{m}=q
,
m\in{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)

for some
{\mathbb{X}}_{q}
-transient set
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)
,
j\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. By
Remark
A.4
,
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)\cap{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)=\varnothing
, and
by Corollary
A.6
,

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)\subset(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k},\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1})
.
Hence, by Lemma
A.8
, there exists
j^{\prime}
such that

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j^{\prime})\,\subset\,(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k},\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1})\,,\quad\text{and}\quad S({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j^{\prime}))\,<\,S({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j))\;.

(A.8)

Suppose that
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j^{\prime})
is a
{\mathbb{X}}_{q}
-transient set. Then, by
Remark
A.4
,
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j^{\prime})\cap{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)=\varnothing
. Therefore, by the first assertion of (
A.8
),

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j^{\prime})
satisfies the hypotheses of Lemma
A.8
. In
particular, as the Markov chain
{\mathbb{X}}_{q}
has a finite number of
states in the interval
(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k},\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1})
,
we may repeat the argument until we find an index
j^{\prime}
such that

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j^{\prime})
belongs to a
{\mathbb{X}}_{q}
-closed irreducible class.

Assume therefore
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j^{\prime})
is
{\mathbb{X}}_{q}
-recurrent. Fix

m^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j^{\prime})
. Since this later state is
{\mathbb{X}}_{q}
-recurrent, by construction,
p_{m^{\prime}}>q=p_{m}
. If
p_{m^{\prime}}\geq p
,
by Lemma
A.3
,
m^{\prime}\not\in{\textsf{M}}_{p}(k)
, so that, by the first
assertion in (
A.8
),
m^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)
. Hence, by the second
assertion in (
A.8
), and postulates
{\mathcal{P}}_{4}(q)
,
{\mathcal{P}}_{4}(p)
,

S({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j))>S({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j^{\prime}))=S(m^{\prime})=S({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k))
, as
claimed.

If
p_{m^{\prime}}<p
, we may iterate the argument until we reach a point

m^{\prime\prime}\in{\mathcal{M}}
such that
p_{m^{\prime\prime}}\geq p
and argue as in the previous
paragraph.
∎

We conclude this section with a result needed in the proof of the
local ergodicity, and one needed in the proof of the convergence of
the solution of the resolvent equation. Note that
\sigma\leq m
in the
first estimate and
m\leq\sigma
in the second one.

Lemma A.9
.

For all
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,

\begin{gathered}\max_{\sigma\in[m^{-}_{p,k},\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1})\cap{\mathcal{W}}}\;\;\max_{m\in[\sigma,\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1}]\cap{\mathcal{M}}}[S(\sigma)-S(m)]\,\leq\,{\mathfrak{h}}_{p-1}\;,\\
\max_{\sigma\in(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k},m^{+}_{p,k}]\cap{\mathcal{W}}}\;\;\max_{m\in[\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k},\sigma]\cap{\mathcal{M}}}[S(\sigma)-S(m)]\,\leq\,{\mathfrak{h}}_{p-1}\;.\end{gathered}

Proof.

We prove the first estimate. Suppose first that

\sigma\in(m^{-}_{p,k},m^{+}_{p,k})\cap{\mathcal{W}}
, and fix

m\in[\sigma,\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1}]
. In this case, by definition of
the energy barrier,

S(\sigma)-S(m)\leq\Lambda(m^{-}_{p,k},m^{+}_{p,k})-S(m)
. By
postulate
{\mathcal{P}}_{5}(p)
, this expression is less than or equal to

S({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k))+{\mathfrak{h}}_{p-1}-S(m)
. By Proposition
A.2
, this
difference is bounded by
{\mathfrak{h}}_{p-1}
, as claimed.

Suppose that
\sigma\in(m^{+}_{p,k},\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1})\cap{\mathcal{W}}
.
Denote by
m
a local minimum of
S(\cdot)
in

(\sigma,\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1})
which attains the minimum value of

S(\cdot)
in this interval. There may be more than one, but this is
irrelevant. Let
q=p_{m}
. This value is smaller than
p
because
m
is
larger than
m^{+}_{p,k}
:
m>\sigma>m^{+}_{p,k}
.

Fix
j\in{\mathbb{Z}}
so that
m\in{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)
and recall the notation
introduced in Lemma
A.7
. We claim that
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)
,

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j_{+})
belong to the same
{\mathbb{X}}_{q}
-equivalent class.

Indeed, by Lemma
A.7
,

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j_{+})\,\subset\,(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k},\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1})\;.

(A.9)

If
{\mathbb{X}}_{q}
is allowed to jump from
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(\ell)
to

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(\ell+1)
but not from
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(\ell+1)
to

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(\ell)
for some
j\leq\ell<j_{+}
, then, by postulate

{\mathcal{P}}_{8}(q)
,

S({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(i))\,<\,S({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j))

(A.10)

for some
j<i\leq j_{+}
.

By postulate
{\mathcal{P}}_{2}(q)
, the sets
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(r)
,
r\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, are
ordered. Thus, as
j<i\leq j_{+}
, by (
A.9
),

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)<{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(i)<\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1}
. This fact and
(
A.10
) contradicts the definition of
m
.

The same argument shows that all set
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(i)
, such that

j_{-}\leq i
and
\sigma<m^{\prime}
for some
m^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(i)
belong to
the
{\mathbb{X}}_{q}
-equivalent class of
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)
. In particular,

S({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(\ell))\,=\,S({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j))
for all

j_{-}\leq\ell\leq j_{+}
such that
m^{\prime\prime}>\sigma
for some

m^{\prime\prime}\in{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(\ell)
.

Recall the definition of
{\mathfrak{s}}(j)
introduced in Lemma
A.8
. By
this result and the previous observation,
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}({\mathfrak{s}}(j))
can
not have elements to the right of
\sigma
because

S({\mathscr{M}}_{q}({\mathfrak{s}}(j)))<S({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j))
. That is,

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}({\mathfrak{s}}(j))<\sigma
. As
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}({\mathfrak{s}}(j))
can be reached
from
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)
with left jumps, by postulates
{\mathcal{P}}_{4}(q)
,

{\mathcal{P}}_{7}(q)
,

S(\sigma)-S(m)=S(\sigma)-S({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j))\leq{\mathfrak{h}}_{q}\leq{\mathfrak{h}}_{p-1}
. This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Remark A.10
.

It might be easier to understand the assertion of Lemma
A.9

rewriting the inequalities as

\begin{gathered}\max_{\sigma\in[m^{p,-}_{k},\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1})\cap{\mathcal{W}}}\Big\{\,S(\sigma)\,-\,\min_{m\in[\sigma,\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1}]\cap{\mathcal{M}}}S(m)\,\Big\}\,\leq\,{\mathfrak{h}}_{p-1}\;,\\
\max_{\sigma\in(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k},m^{p,+}_{k}]\cap{\mathcal{W}}}\Big\{\,S(\sigma)\,-\,\min_{m\in[\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k},\sigma]\cap{\mathcal{M}}}S(m)\,\Big\}\,\leq\,{\mathfrak{h}}_{p-1}\end{gathered}

for all
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,

Recall the definition of
\sigma^{p,\pm}_{k,k+1}
,
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,
introduced in (
A.5
). The case
p=1
is excluded in the next lemma
because
\sigma^{1,-}_{k,k+1}=\sigma^{1,+}_{k,k+1}
for all

k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. In the proof of this result, the
{\mathbb{X}}_{q}
-equivalent
classes are to be understood in the sense of Markov chains and not in
the sense of the equivalence relation introduced at the beginning of
Section
2
.

Lemma A.11
.

Fix
2\leq p\leq{\mathfrak{q}}
,
k\in Z
, and suppose that
\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1}<\sigma^{p,+}_{k,k+1}
. Then,

\min\big\{S(x):x\in[\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1}\,,\,\sigma^{p,+}_{k,k+1}]\,\big\}\,>\,S({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k))\;.

Proof.

Let
m\in[\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1}\,,\,\sigma^{p,+}_{k,k+1}]
such that

S(m)\,=\,\min\big\{S(x):x\in[\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1}\,,\,\sigma^{p,+}_{k,k+1}]\,\big\}\;,

and suppose that
S(m)\leq S({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k))
. We obtain a contradiction
showing that, under this condition,
m\in{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(i)
for some

i\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.

Fix
q<p
and suppose that
m\in{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)
for some
j\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. Recall the definition of the indices
j_{\pm}
introduced in the
proof of Lemma
A.7
. Since
S(m)\leq S({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k))
, by the
proof of Lemma
A.7
,

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j_{-})\cup{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j_{+})\subset(\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1}\,,\,\sigma^{p,+}_{k,k+1})
. [Here we use the fact that

S({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j))=S(m)\leq S({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k))\leq S(\sigma^{p,+}_{k,k+1})-{\mathfrak{h}}_{p}
.]

Let
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(i)
,
r_{-}\leq i\leq r_{+}
be the
{\mathbb{X}}_{q}
-equivalent class
of
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)
. In particular,
j_{-}\leq r_{-}\leq j\leq r_{+}\leq j_{+}
. By
the previous paragraph, the
{\mathbb{X}}_{q}
-equivalent class of

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)
is contained in

(\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1}\,,\,\sigma^{p,+}_{k,k+1})
.

Thus,
m\in{\mathscr{M}}_{q+1}(j^{\prime})
for some
j^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. Iterating this
argument yields that
m\in{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(j^{\prime\prime})
for some
j^{\prime\prime}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, in
contradiction with the definition of
\sigma^{p,+}_{k,k+1}
[as the
largest local maximum separating
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)
from

{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k+1)
].
∎

Appendix B
The resolvent equation

In this section, we prove the existence and uniqueness of the
resolvent equation weak solution, as well as its stochastic
representation. We refer to
[
7
]
for the definitions omitted
below. Let
I
be an open interval of
{\mathbb{R}}
, which can be equal to

{\mathbb{R}}
. Denote by
L_{loc}(I)
the set of Lebesgue locally
integrable functions
f\colon I\to{\mathbb{R}}
. Let
C_{c}^{\infty}(I)
be
the space smooth functions
f\colon I\to{\mathbb{R}}
with compact support.

Given a positive integer
\alpha\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{+}
, let

{\mathcal{H}}^{\alpha}(I)
be the Sobolev space of
L^{2}(I)
functions
u

such that, for each
1\leq k\leq\alpha
, the
k
-th weak derivative
of
u
, denoted by
\partial_{x}^{k}u
, exists and belongs to

L^{2}(I)
. For
u\in{\mathcal{H}}^{\alpha}(I)
, define the norm

\lVert u\rVert^{2}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\alpha}(I)}:=\lVert u\rVert_{L^{2}(I)}^{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{\alpha}\,\lVert\partial_{x}^{k}u\rVert_{L^{2}(I)}^{2}\,.

Denote as
{\mathcal{H}}_{loc}^{\alpha}(I)
the space of locally integrable
functions
u\in L_{loc}(I)
such that, for every finite open set

J\subset I
, the restriction of
u
to
J
belongs to

{\mathcal{H}}^{\alpha}(J)
.

Definition B.1

(Weak solution)
.

Let
F\colon I\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a bounded measurable function. We say that
a function
\phi\in{\mathcal{H}}^{1}_{loc}(I)
is a weak solution of the
resolvent equation

(\lambda-\vartheta_{\epsilon}\,{\mathscr{L}}_{\epsilon})\,\phi=F,

(B.1)

if

\displaystyle\lambda\,\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\,\phi(x)\,\psi(x)\,dx\,+\,\epsilon\,\vartheta_{\epsilon}\,\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\,\partial_{x}\phi(x)\,\partial_{x}\big[{\textsf{a}}(x)\psi(x)\big]\,dx\,-\,\vartheta_{\epsilon}\,\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\textsf{b}}(x)\,\partial_{x}\phi(x)\,\psi(x)\,dx

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\,F(x)\,\psi(x)\,dx\,,

(B.2)

holds for all
\psi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(I)
.

It is sometimes convenient to rewrite this equation as follows. Take
as test function
\psi=\hat{\psi}e^{-S/\epsilon}/{\textsf{a}}
, for some

\hat{\psi}\in C_{c}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}})
, and recall the definition of
S

to obtain that

\displaystyle\lambda\,\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\,\frac{1}{{\textsf{a}}(x)}\,\phi(x)\,\hat{\psi}(x)\,e^{-S(x)/\epsilon}\,dx\,+\,\vartheta_{\epsilon}\,\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\,\partial_{x}\phi(x)\,\partial_{x}\hat{\psi}(x)\,e^{-S(x)/\epsilon}\,\,dx\,

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\frac{1}{{\textsf{a}}(x)}\,F(x)\,\hat{\psi}(x)\,e^{-S(x)/\epsilon}\,dx\,.

Let
I_{r}=(-r,r)
,
r>0
. Fix a bounded measurable function

G\colon I_{r}\to{\mathbb{R}}
,
\lambda>0
and constants
{\mathfrak{c}}_{\pm}
. By
Theorem 6.2.3 with
\gamma=0
and the Remark just after the definition
of weak solutions in Section 6.1 of
[
7
]
, there exists a unique
solution
u\in{\mathcal{H}}^{1}(I_{r})
to the Dirichlet
problem

\left\{\begin{aligned} &(\lambda-\vartheta_{\epsilon}{\mathscr{L}}_{\epsilon})\,u=H\quad\text{in}\;\;I_{r}\,,\\
&u(\pm r)={\mathfrak{c}}_{\pm}\,.\end{aligned}\right.

(B.3)

Clearly, if
H=\lambda\,{\mathfrak{c}}
, for some constant
{\mathfrak{c}}\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
and
{\mathfrak{c}}_{\pm}={\mathfrak{c}}
, the unique solution is
u={\mathfrak{c}}
. This solution is
denoted by
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u_{r,{\mathfrak{c}}}
.

Suppose that
v\in{\mathcal{H}}^{1}(I_{s})
is a weak solution of (
B.1
) in

I_{s}
(in other words, (
B.2
) holds for all

\psi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(I_{s})
). Then, by
[
7
, Theorem 6.3.1]
, for
all
0<r<s
, there exists a finite constant

C_{r}=C_{r}(\epsilon,s,\lambda,{\textsf{a}},{\textsf{b}})
such that

\|v\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{2}(I_{r})}\,\leq\,C_{r}\,(\|H\|_{L^{2}(I_{s})}+\|v\|_{L^{2}(I_{s})})\;.

(B.4)

Suppose that
H
in (
B.3
) is uniformly Hölder continuous and

{\mathfrak{c}}_{\pm}=0
. By the stochastic representation of the solution of
elliptic equations,
[
12
, Theorem 6.5.1]
,

u(x)\;=\;{\mathbb{E}}^{\epsilon,\vartheta_{\epsilon}}_{x}\Big[\,\int_{0}^{\tau_{r}}e^{-\lambda t}\,H({\mathtt{x}}(t))\,dt\,\Big]\;,

(B.5)

where
\tau_{r}
is the hitting time of the boundary of

(-r,r)
.

We turn to the existence of solutions of (
1.6
), obtained as the
limit of solutions of Dirichlet problems. By linearity, it is enough
to prove the existence for positive functions
G
. Denote by
u_{n}

the unique solution to the Dirichlet problem (
B.3
) with
r=n
,

{\mathfrak{c}}_{\pm}=0
,
H=G
.

Approximate
G
from above and from below by sequences of bounded
uniformly Hölder continuous functions
G_{k,+}
,
G_{k,-}
in such a
way that
G_{k,-}\leq G\leq G_{k,+}
,

\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}}[\,G_{k,+}(x)-G_{k,-}(x)\,]\leq 1/k
. Denote
by
u_{n,k,\pm}
the unique solution to the Dirichlet problem
(
B.3
) with
r=n
,
{\mathfrak{c}}_{\pm}=0
,
H=G_{k,\pm}
. By the maximum
principle
[
13
, Theorem 8.1]
,
u_{n,k,-}\leq u_{n}\leq u_{n,k,+}
. Hence, by (
B.5
) for
u_{n,k,\pm}
and the dominated
convergence theorem,

u_{n}(x)\;=\;{\mathbb{E}}^{\epsilon,\vartheta_{\epsilon}}_{x}\Big[\,\int_{0}^{\tau_{n}}e^{-\lambda t}\,G({\mathtt{x}}(t))\,dt\,\Big]\;.

(B.6)

Thus, the stochastic representation of the solution can be extended to
positive linear combination of sets indicators. Moreover, by this
formula, for all
n_{0}\geq 1
,

\lim_{m_{0}\to\infty}\sup_{n,m\geq m_{0}}\sup_{x\in I_{n_{0}}}\big|\,u_{n}(x)-u_{m}(x)\,\big|\,=\,0\;.

(B.7)

By (
B.7
), (
B.4
), for each
n_{0}\geq 1
, the sequence

(u_{n}:n\geq 1)
is Cauchy in
L^{\infty}(I_{n_{0}})
and in

{\mathcal{H}}^{2}(I_{n_{0}})
. By a standard argument one concludes the existence
of a bounded solution to (
B.1
) in
{\mathcal{H}}^{2}_{\rm loc}({\mathbb{R}})

which can be represented as

u(x)\;=\;{\mathbb{E}}^{\epsilon,\vartheta_{\epsilon}}_{x}\Big[\,\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\lambda t}\,G({\mathtt{x}}(t))\,dt\,\Big]\;.

(B.8)

In particular,

\|u\|_{\infty}\leq\lambda^{-1}\|G\|_{\infty}
.

A bounded solution to (
B.1
) in
{\mathcal{H}}^{2}_{\rm loc}
is unique.
Indeed, let
u_{1}
,
u_{2}
be two bounded solutions to (
B.1
) in

{\mathcal{H}}^{2}_{\rm loc}
, so that
v=u_{1}-u_{2}
is a bounded solution to
(
B.1
) in
{\mathcal{H}}^{2}_{\rm loc}
with
H=0
. Fix
n\geq 1
. The
solution
v
restricted to
I_{n}
solves the Dirichlet problem
(
B.3
) with
H=0
and boundary conditions
{\mathfrak{c}}_{\pm}=v(\pm n)
.
By the stochastic representation of the solution of elliptic
equations,
[
12
, Theorem 6.5.1]
,

v(x)\;=\;{\mathbb{E}}^{\epsilon,\vartheta_{\epsilon}}_{x}\Big[\,v({\mathtt{x}}(\tau_{n}))\,e^{-\lambda\tau_{n}}\,\Big]\;,\quad x\in I_{n}\;.

As
v
is bounded, for each fixed
x\in{\mathbb{R}}
, the right-hand side
vanishes as
n\to\infty
. This proves that
v=0
as claimed.
